<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a stable radial pair potential in three dimensions whose canonical free energy has a strict downward derivative jump at one common finite positive inverse temperature throughout an open interval of positive densities. The potential has a divergent repulsive core, a nontrivial attractive interval, and an integrable tail satisfying $`\phi(r)=o(r^{-3})`$. Its free-cube canonical free energy is finite at every positive inverse temperature and density.

- **claim-main-temperature-singularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist an admissible radial difference-only pair potential $\Phi(x)=\phi(\|x\|)$ on $\mathbb R^3$, a nonempty open interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$, and one $\beta_c\in(1/2,3/2)$ with the following properties. The potential satisfies $\phi(r)=o(r^{-3})$ as $r\to\infty$. The free-cube limit <a href="#eq:free-energy-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:free-energy-definition">[eq:free-energy-definition]</a> exists and is finite for every $\beta>0$ and every density $\rho>0$. For every $\rho\in I$, the finite one-sided derivatives at the same critical point satisfy $$D_\beta^- f_\Phi(\beta_c,\rho) >D_\beta^+ f_\Phi(\beta_c,\rho).$$ Thus one potential and one critical inverse temperature give a strict canonical temperature singularity throughout $I$.
  - Explanation: One engineered radial potential with a divergent core and an integrable tail has a single critical inverse temperature at which the canonical free energy has a strict downward jump of its temperature derivative, for every density in an open interval.
- **claim-shell-graph-clique-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shell-graph)): No five points in $\mathbb R^3$ have all their mutual distances in $[e_t,(1+\delta)e_t]$. Consequently, a graph whose vertices are points of $\mathbb R^3$ and whose edges join pairs at these distances has at most $3n^2/8$ edges on any set of $n$ vertices.
  - Explanation: Five points cannot be mutually at shell distances in three dimensions, so a shell graph has no five-vertex complete subgraph and its edge count is bounded by a quadratic in the number of vertices.
- **claim-uniform-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-stability)): Put $A_0=E/8$ and $B_0=E/2$. Every potential in <a href="#eq:physical-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-class">[eq:physical-class]</a> satisfies, for every finite configuration, $$\sum_{\ell<k}\psi_t(x_\ell-x_k) \ge A_0\sum_i n_i^2-B_0N, \qquad U_N^\varphi\ge (A_0-M/2)\sum_i n_i^2-B_0N. \label{eq:superstable}$$ These inequalities remain valid if positive core interactions between members of a partition into unions of whole cells are deleted, or if any negative interactions are multiplied by factors in $[0,1]$.
  - Explanation: The core energy gives a quadratic lower bound in the cell occupation numbers, and the attractive part costs at most a smaller quadratic. The bounds survive deleting core interactions between unions of cells or damping negative interactions.
- **claim-canonical-grand-canonical-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-thermodynamics)): For every $\beta>0$ and $d\ge0$ there is a finite limit $$H_\varphi(\beta,d) =-\lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\n/L^3\to d}} \frac{\log Z_{L,n}(\beta)}{tL^3}, \qquad H_\varphi(\beta,0)=0. \label{eq:canonical-limit}$$ Extend $H_\varphi(\beta,d)$ by $+\infty$ for $d<0$. It is closed and convex in $d$, and finite and concave in $\beta$ for every $d\ge0$. Moreover, $$H_\varphi(\beta,d) \ge \frac{\beta\kappa}{t}d^2 -\frac{\beta B_0}{t}d +\frac{d\log d-d}{t} \qquad(d\ge0). \label{eq:coercivity}$$ Let $\Omega=(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R$, with coordinates $(\beta,h)$, and define $$\Xi_L^\varphi(\beta,h) =\sum_{n\ge0}e^{thn}Z_{L,n}(\beta).$$ Then the finite convex pressure is $$Q_\varphi(\beta,h) =\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\log\Xi_L^\varphi(\beta,h)}{tL^3} =\sup_{d\ge0}\{hd-H_\varphi(\beta,d)\}. \label{eq:pressure-duality}$$ The convergence is uniform on compact subsets of $\Omega$, and $$H_\varphi(\beta,d) =\sup_{h\in\mathbb R}\{hd-Q_\varphi(\beta,h)\} \qquad(d\in\mathbb R). \label{eq:partial-conjugacy}$$ The bounds above use the same constants for the entire class <a href="#eq:physical-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-class">[eq:physical-class]</a> with the chosen $t$.
  - Explanation: The reduced canonical free energy exists along all sequences with a prescribed density, is convex in density and concave in inverse temperature, satisfies a coercive lower bound, and is Fenchel-dual to the grand-canonical pressure with locally uniform convergence.
- **claim-density-transform-bounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-transform)): Let $t$ satisfy <a href="#eq:t-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:t-choice">[eq:t-choice]</a>. For every physical pressure $Q_\varphi$ in <a href="#eq:physical-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-class">[eq:physical-class]</a> and every $\sigma\in[0,A+1]$, the function $T_\sigma Q_\varphi$ is finite and convex on $\Omega$, nonnegative, and nondecreasing in $h$. The supremum in <a href="#eq:transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transform">[eq:transform]</a> is attained. For every compact $W\subset\Omega$, there is $D_W<\infty$, depending only on $W,t,b,w,A$, such that every maximizing density belongs to $[0,D_W]$, uniformly over all potentials in the class and all these values of $\sigma$. The transformed pressures are also uniformly bounded on $W$.
  - Explanation: The quadratic density transform of a physical pressure is finite, convex, nonnegative and monotone in the field, its supremum is attained, and maximizing densities are bounded uniformly over the class on compact parameter sets.
- **claim-uniform-local-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-moments)): Fix a compact set $\mathcal K\subset\Omega$. For all potentials in <a href="#eq:physical-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-class">[eq:physical-class]</a>, all finite cubes, and all the modified systems just described, there is a constant $C_{\mathcal K}$ such that $$\mathbb E_{\beta,h}[n_i n_j]\le C_{\mathcal K} \qquad ((\beta,h)\in\mathcal K)$$ for every pair of cells meeting the cube. The constant is independent of the number and ranges of the attractive summands.
  - Explanation: Second moments of pairs of cell occupations in the grand-canonical measure are bounded uniformly in the volume, the number and ranges of attractive summands, and the permitted modifications of the system.
- **claim-attractive-perturbation-tail** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tail)): Fix a compact set $\mathcal K\subset\Omega$. For two of the preceding finite-volume systems differing by a negative interaction, let $J^{\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}$ be cellwise suprema of its absolute value. If the linear interpolation stays within the same attractive mass bound, then $$\label{eq:pressure-interpolation} \|\log\Xi(1)-\log\Xi(0)\| \le \frac{\beta C_{\mathcal K}}2\sum_{i,j}J^{\mathrm{diff}}_{ij} \qquad ((\beta,h)\in\mathcal K).$$ In particular, for $$\varphi=\psi_t-t\sum_{j\ge1}\sigma_jw_{R_j}, \qquad \varphi_n=\psi_t-t\sum_{j\le n}\sigma_jw_{R_j},$$ there is a range-independent constant $C'_{\mathcal K}$ such that $$\label{eq:tail-continuity} \\|Q_\varphi-Q_{\varphi_n}\\|_{\mathcal K} \le C'_{\mathcal K}\sum_{j>n}\sigma_j .$$
  - Explanation: The change of the logarithmic grand partition function under an attractive perturbation is controlled by the cellwise size of the perturbation, which gives a range-independent bound on the pressure difference caused by dropping a tail of attractive terms.
- **claim-physical-kac-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-kac)): Let $$\varphi=\psi_t-t\sum_{j=1}^k\sigma_jw_{R_j}$$ have finitely many attractive summands, with the empty sum allowed. Fix $\sigma\ge0$ such that $\sum_{j=1}^k\sigma_j+\sigma\le A+1$. Then $$\label{eq:kac-limit} Q_{\varphi-t\sigma w_R}\longrightarrow T_\sigma Q_\varphi \quad\text{as }R\to\infty$$ uniformly on compact subsets of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Adding one attraction of growing range to a fixed finite-range reference makes the pressure converge locally uniformly to the quadratic density transform of the reference pressure.
- **claim-reference-pressure-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-free-pressure)): Uniformly on compact subsets of $\Omega$, $$\label{eq:free-pressure-limit} q_t(\beta,h)\longrightarrow q_*(h):=\max(0,ph,4ph-9p).$$
  - Explanation: As the core scale grows, the reference pressure tends to the maximum of three affine branches for empty space, separated single particles and four-particle clusters.
- **claim-ideal-transform-three-planes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-initial-transform)): Uniformly on compact subsets of $\Omega$, $$\label{eq:ideal-three-planes} P_1^t(\beta,h)\longrightarrow P_*(\beta,h) :=\max\left(0,\ ph+\frac{\beta Ap^2}{2},\ 4ph-9p+8\beta Ap^2\right).$$ Moreover, if $t_j\to\infty$, $(\beta_j,h_j)\to(\beta,h)\in\Omega$, and $d_j$ maximizes the expression defining $P_1^{t_j}(\beta_j,h_j)$, then the $d_j$ are bounded. Every subsequential limit belongs to $\{0,p,4p\}$ and maximizes the corresponding limiting expression.
  - Explanation: The ideal transform of the reference pressure converges to a maximum of three planes, and limits of maximizing densities lie in a three-point set and maximize the limiting expression.
- **claim-joint-fenchel-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-joint-support)): Let $Q$ be finite and convex on $\Omega$, and let $$H(\beta,d)=\sup_{h\in\mathbb R}\{hd-Q(\beta,h)\}.$$ Fix $d\ge0$ for which $H(\,\cdot\,,d)$ is finite and concave. If $(\beta_0,z)\in\Omega$ satisfies $$H(\beta_0,d)=zd-Q(\beta_0,z),$$ then $$\label{eq:joint-support-identity} \{e\in\mathbb R:(e,d)\in\partial Q(\beta_0,z)\} =-\partial_\beta^+H(\beta_0,d).$$
  - Explanation: At a field where Fenchel equality holds, the first coordinates of joint subgradients of the pressure with a fixed density equal the negative superdifferential of the canonical function in inverse temperature.
- **claim-support-under-quadratic-transform** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transform-support)): Let $t$ satisfy <a href="#eq:t-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:t-choice">[eq:t-choice]</a>, let $Q=Q_\varphi$ for a potential in <a href="#eq:physical-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:physical-class">[eq:physical-class]</a>, and let $0<a\le A+1$. Put $H=H_Q$ and $F=T_aQ$. Suppose that $d\ge0$ attains the supremum defining $F(\beta,h)$ at $(\beta,h)\in\Omega$, and put $z=h+\beta ad$. Then, for every $d'\ge0$, $$\label{eq:quadratic-density-support} H(\beta,d')\ge H(\beta,d)+z(d'-d) +\frac{\beta a}{2}(d'-d)^2.$$ The finite concave function $H(\,\cdot\,,d)$ has a supergradient at $\beta$. For every $e\in-\partial_\beta^+H(\beta,d)$, $$\label{eq:transform-support-transfer} (e,d)\in\partial Q(\beta,z),\qquad \left(e+\frac{ad^2}{2},d\right)\in\partial F(\beta,h).$$ If $F$ is differentiable at $(\beta,h)$, the second vector equals $\nabla F(\beta,h)$.
  - Explanation: A density maximizing the quadratic transform gives a supporting vector of the original pressure at a shifted field and a shifted supporting vector of the transform, with the first slope moved by an explicit increment.
- **claim-initial-supporting-triple** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-initial-triple)): For all sufficiently large $t$, there are $\lambda_1\in B(\lambda_*,r_0/16)$ and nonempty compact convex sets $K_i^1\subset\overline B(v_i,\tau/2)$, $i=1,2,3$, such that $$\label{eq:initial-triple} g\in\partial P_1^t(\lambda_1) =\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\bigl(K_1^1\cup K_2^1\cup K_3^1\bigr).$$ All vectors in the $K_i^1$ have nonnegative density coordinate. In particular $\partial P_1^t(\lambda_1)$ contains $\overline B(g,\gamma)$.
  - Explanation: For a large core scale the subdifferential of the ideal transform at a point near the planes' common point is the hull of three nonempty groups near the three plane gradients, and it contains a fixed ball.
- **claim-persistence-three-groups** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-persistence)): Let $W\subset\Omega$ be compact, let $P$ be finite and convex on $\Omega$, and let $\lambda_0=(\beta_0,h_0)\in\operatorname{int}W$. Suppose that $$\partial P(\lambda_0)=K=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}(K_1\cup K_2\cup K_3), \qquad B(g,\gamma)\subset K, \label{eq:persistence-input}$$ where $g\in\mathbb R^2$, $\gamma>0$, and the $K_i$ are nonempty compact convex sets whose projections onto the density coordinate are nonnegative and strictly ordered. Let $\zeta>0$ be such that the sets $K_i+\overline B(0,\zeta)$ still have disjoint density projections, and $g$ lies outside the convex hull of any two of these enlarged sets.* *There exist $S<\infty$ and $a_*>0$ with the following property. For every $0<a<a_*$ and every physical pressure $Q$ satisfying $$\\|Q-P\\|_W\leq a^2, \label{eq:persistence-error}$$ there is a point $\lambda'\in\operatorname{int}W$ with $\|\lambda'-\lambda_0\|\leq Sa$ such that $$g\in\partial(T_aQ)(\lambda') =\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}(J_1\cup J_2\cup J_3). \label{eq:persistence-output}$$ Here each $J_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, has nonnegative density projection, and satisfies $J_i\subset K_i+\overline B(0,\zeta)$. The constants may depend on the displayed data and the fixed physical class, but not on $Q$. We may require $a_*\leq\min(1,A+1)$.
  - Explanation: If a physical pressure approximates a pressure with three separated gradient groups to within the square of a small mass, then adding an attraction of that mass keeps three separated groups at a nearby point containing the same target slope.
- **claim-local-convex-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-convex-convergence)): Let $O\subset\mathbb R^m$ be open and convex. A finite convex function $F$ on $O$ is locally Lipschitz and has a nonempty compact subdifferential at every point of $O$. Its subgradients are bounded on compact subsets. If finite convex functions $F_n$ converge pointwise on $O$ to a finite function $F$, then $F$ is convex and the convergence is locally uniform. Moreover, if $x_n\to x\in O$, $F_n\to F$ locally uniformly, and $v_n\in\partial F_n(x_n)$, then $(v_n)$ is bounded and every limit belongs to $\partial F(x)$.
  - Explanation: Finite convex functions are locally Lipschitz with compact subdifferentials, pointwise convergence upgrades to locally uniform convergence, and limits of subgradients are subgradients of the limit.
- **claim-one-dimensional-conjugacy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-elementary-conjugacy)): Let $u:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$ be proper, lower semicontinuous, and convex, with a domain having nonempty interior. Then $$u(d)=\sup_{h\in\mathbb R}\{hd-u^*(h)\},\qquad u^*(h)=\sup_{d\in\mathbb R}\{hd-u(d)\}.$$ At a finite point $d$, equality $u(d)+u^*(h)=hd$ holds precisely when $u(d')\ge u(d)+h(d'-d)$ for every $d'$. In particular, if $K\subset\mathbb R^2$ is nonempty compact convex with a density projection of positive length, then for every $x\in\mathbb R$, $$\max\{xs:(s,d)\in K\} =\inf_{z\in\mathbb R}\{h_K(x,z)-zd\},$$ where the maximum is $-\infty$ outside that projection.
  - Explanation: A proper closed convex function on the line equals its biconjugate, Fenchel equality characterizes supporting slopes, and the slice maximum of a compact convex planar set is recovered from its support function.
- **claim-reachable-gradients-planar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-reachable-local)): Let $F$ be finite and convex on an open convex subset $O\subset\mathbb R^2$. For $x\in O$, let $G_F(x)$ be the limits of gradients at differentiability points tending to $x$. Then $G_F(x)$ is nonempty compact and $$\partial F(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}G_F(x).$$ For every $e\in\mathbb R^2$, $$\lim_{a\downarrow0}\frac{F(x+ae)-F(x)}a =\max_{v\in\partial F(x)}v\cdot e.$$ The rescaled functions in this last display converge uniformly for $e$ in compact subsets of $\mathbb R^2$.
  - Explanation: For a planar finite convex function the subdifferential is the convex hull of the reachable gradients, and directional difference quotients converge uniformly to the support function of the subdifferential.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a compact set $\\mathcal K\\subset\\Omega$. If the linear interpolation stays within the same attractive mass bound, then $$\\label{eq:pressure-interpolation}\n |\\log\\Xi(1)-\\log\\Xi(0)|\n \\le \\frac{\\beta C_{\\mathcal K}}2\\sum_{i,j}J^{\\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal K).$$ In particular, for $$\\varphi=\\psi_t-t\\sum_{j\\ge1}\\sigma_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad\n \\varphi_n=\\psi_t-t\\sum_{j\\le n}\\sigma_jw_{R_j},$$ there is a range-independent constant $C'_{\\mathcal K}$ such that $$\\label{eq:tail-continuity}\n \\|Q_\\varphi-Q_{\\varphi_n}\\|_{\\mathcal K}\n \\le C'_{\\mathcal K}\\sum_{j>n}\\sigma_j .$$",
        "source_id": "PM-228.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-persistence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W\\subset\\Omega$ be compact, let $P$ be finite and convex on $\\Omega$, and let $\\lambda_0=(\\beta_0,h_0)\\in\\operatorname{int}W$. Suppose that $$\\partial P(\\lambda_0)=K=\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}(K_1\\cup K_2\\cup K_3),\n \\qquad B(g,\\gamma)\\subset K,\n \\label{eq:persistence-input}$$ where $g\\in\\mathbb R^2$, $\\gamma>0$, and the $K_i$ are nonempty compact convex sets whose projections onto the density coordinate are nonnegative and strictly ordered. Let $\\zeta>0$ be such that the sets $K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$ still have disjoint density projections, and $g$ lies outside the convex hull of any two of these enlarged sets.*\n\n*There exist $S<\\infty$ and $a_*>0$ with the following property. For every $0<a<a_*$ and every physical pressure $Q$ satisfying $$\\|Q-P\\|_W\\leq a^2,\n \\label{eq:persistence-error}$$ there is a point $\\lambda'\\in\\operatorname{int}W$ with $|\\lambda'-\\lambda_0|\\leq Sa$ such that $$g\\in\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda')\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}(J_1\\cup J_2\\cup J_3).\n \\label{eq:persistence-output}$$ Here each $J_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, has nonnegative density projection, and satisfies $J_i\\subset K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$. The constants may depend on the displayed data and the fixed physical class, but not on $Q$. We may require $a_*\\leq\\min(1,A+1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-persistence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W\\subset\\Omega$ be compact, let $P$ be finite and convex on $\\Omega$, and let $\\lambda_0=(\\beta_0,h_0)\\in\\operatorname{int}W$. Let $\\zeta>0$ be such that the sets $K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$ still have disjoint density projections, and $g$ lies outside the convex hull of any two of these enlarged sets.*\n\n*There exist $S<\\infty$ and $a_*>0$ with the following property. For every $0<a<a_*$ and every physical pressure $Q$ satisfying $$\\|Q-P\\|_W\\leq a^2,\n \\label{eq:persistence-error}$$ there is a point $\\lambda'\\in\\operatorname{int}W$ with $|\\lambda'-\\lambda_0|\\leq Sa$ such that $$g\\in\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda')\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}(J_1\\cup J_2\\cup J_3).\n \\label{eq:persistence-output}$$ Here each $J_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, has nonnegative density projection, and satisfies $J_i\\subset K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$. The constants may depend on the displayed data and the fixed physical class, but not on $Q$. We may require $a_*\\leq\\min(1,A+1)$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $W\\subset\\Omega$ be compact, let $P$ be finite and convex on $\\Omega$, and let $\\lambda_0=(\\beta_0,h_0)\\in\\operatorname{int}W$. Suppose that $$\\partial P(\\lambda_0)=K=\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}(K_1\\cup K_2\\cup K_3),\n \\qquad B(g,\\gamma)\\subset K,\n \\label{eq:persistence-input}$$ where $g\\in\\mathbb R^2$, $\\gamma>0$, and the $K_i$ are nonempty compact convex sets whose projections onto the density coordinate are nonnegative and strictly ordered. Let $\\zeta>0$ be such that the sets $K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$ still have disjoint density projections, and $g$ lies outside the convex hull of any two of these enlarged sets.*\n\n*There exist $S<\\infty$ and $a_*>0$ with the following property. For every $0<a<a_*$ and every physical pressure $Q$ satisfying $$\\|Q-P\\|_W\\leq a^2,\n \\label{eq:persistence-error}$$ there is a point $\\lambda'\\in\\operatorname{int}W$ with $|\\lambda'-\\lambda_0|\\leq Sa$ such that $$g\\in\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda')\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}(J_1\\cup J_2\\cup J_3).\n \\label{eq:persistence-output}$$ Here each $J_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, has nonnegative density projection, and satisfies $J_i\\subset K_i+\\overline B(0,\\zeta)$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-elementary-convex-convergence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $O\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be open and convex. A finite convex function $F$ on $O$ is locally Lipschitz and has a nonempty compact subdifferential at every point of $O$. Its subgradients are bounded on compact subsets. If finite convex functions $F_n$ converge pointwise on $O$ to a finite function $F$, then $F$ is convex and the convergence is locally uniform. Moreover, if $x_n\\to x\\in O$, $F_n\\to F$ locally uniformly, and $v_n\\in\\partial F_n(x_n)$, then $(v_n)$ is bounded and every limit belongs to $\\partial F(x)$.",

Then $$u(d)=\\sup_{h\\in\\mathbb R}\\{hd-u^*(h)\\},\\qquad\n u^*(h)=\\sup_{d\\in\\mathbb R}\\{hd-u(d)\\}.$$ At a finite point $d$, equality $u(d)+u^*(h)=hd$ holds precisely when $u(d')\\ge u(d)+h(d'-d)$ for every $d'$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **admissible potential** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A potential $\Phi(x)=\phi(\|x\|)$ is *admissible* if it is Borel measurable, $\Phi(0)=+\infty$, and $\phi$ is finite and locally bounded on $(0,\infty)$, with the following properties:
- **free-boundary cube** ([source](#eq:free-energy-definition)): Let $\Lambda_L=[-L/2,L/2]^3$.
- **shell parameters** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Fix $$\delta=10^{-3},\qquad b=\frac14.$$
- **core scales E and e_t** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a parameter $t>1$, put $E=t^2$ and $e_t=e^{-t}$.
- **packing number M_L** ([source](#eq:packing-density)): Let $M_L$ be the largest cardinality of a subset of $\Lambda_L=[-L/2,L/2]^3$ whose distinct points have distance at least one.
- **attractive kernel w_R** ([source](#eq:physical-class)): For $R\ge1$ set $w_R(x)=R^{-3}w(x/R)$.
- **cell partition** ([source](#eq:cell-row)): Partition $\mathbb R^3$ into half-open cubes $C_i$ of side $b$, indexed by $i\in\mathbb Z^3$.
- **sup norm on compact set** ([source](#eq:free-energy-normalization)): For a real-valued function $F$ on a compact set $K$, write $\\|F\\|_K=\sup_{x\in K}\|F(x)\|$.
- **bad edge** ([source](#eq:bad-particle-factor)): A bad edge is a pair at distance below one but outside the allowed shell.
- **subdifferential** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a finite convex function $F$ on an open convex set, write $\partial F(\lambda)$ for the vectors $v$ satisfying $F(y)\ge F(\lambda)+v\cdot(y-\lambda)$ throughout that set.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-shell-thickness | relative shell thickness delta | = | $10^{-3}$ | fixed throughout the paper |
| q-cell-side | cell side b | = | $\frac14$ | fixed throughout the paper |
| q-core-energy | core energy scale E | = | $t^2$ | scale parameter t greater than one |
| q-edge-count-bound | edges of a shell graph on n vertices | ≤ | $3n^2/8$ | any number n of vertices |
| q-gram-perturbation | operator norm of Gram matrix deviation | = | $0.008004$ | shell width fixed above |
| q-attraction-ceiling | attraction mass parameter A | = | $\frac{3}{2p}$ | paper-wide |
| q-stability-quadratic | quadratic stability coefficient A_0 | = | $E/8$ | physical class at fixed t |
| q-stability-linear | linear stability coefficient B_0 | = | $E/2$ | physical class at fixed t |
| q-coercivity-condition | net coercivity constant kappa | > | $10t(A+1)$ | t sufficiently large |
| q-reference-pressure | limiting reference pressure | = | $\max(0,ph,4ph-9p)$ | field h in compact sets |
| q-slope-barycenter | barycenter g of the three plane gradients | = | $\left(\frac{17p}{4},\frac{5p}{3}\right)$ | depends on packing density p |
| q-center-density | center density rho_* | = | $\frac{5p}{3}$ | depends on packing density p |
| q-tail-estimate | scaled tail of the potential | ≤ | $t\\|w\\|_\infty\sum_{R_j\ge r}\sigma_j$ | r at least one, tending to infinity |
| q-canonical-jump | jump of the reduced canonical function in inverse temperature | ≥ | $\gamma$ | every density in the interval |
| q-free-energy-jump | jump of the free-energy derivative | ≥ | $\frac{t\gamma}{\beta_c}$ | every density in the interval |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-shell-graph** [claim](#stmt-lem-shell-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shell-graph): Gram matrix perturbation excludes five shell points; weighted Motzkin-Straus argument bounds edges. -> No five-clique and an edge bound for shell graphs.
- **step-uniform-stability** [claim](#stmt-prop-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stability): Count shell pairs inside each cell, charge the rest the core energy, bound the attraction by cell row sums. -> Quadratic lower bound for the energy.
- **step-convex-convergence** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-convex-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-convex-convergence): Vertex bounds give local Lipschitz estimates; separating hyperplanes give subgradients. -> Locally uniform convergence and subgradient limits.
- **step-conjugacy** [claim](#stmt-lem-elementary-conjugacy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-elementary-conjugacy): Strict separation of points below the epigraph yields biconjugacy. -> Biconjugacy and slice formula.
- **step-reachable-gradients** [claim](#stmt-lem-reachable-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reachable-local): Almost-everywhere differentiability and limits of gradients give the hull formula. -> Subdifferential as hull of reachable gradients.
- **step-canonical-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-thermodynamics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-thermodynamics): Trial bound, lower bound from stability, separated-box subadditivity, then Laplace-type duality. -> Canonical limit, convexity, concavity and pressure duality.
- **step-density-transform** [claim](#stmt-prop-transform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-transform): Coercivity of the canonical function against the added quadratic bounds maximizing densities. -> Finite convex transform with attained supremum.
- **step-local-moments** [claim](#stmt-prop-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-moments): Conditional exponential moment bound followed by a finite-volume bootstrap. -> Uniform second moments of cell occupations.
- **step-tail-continuity** [claim](#stmt-prop-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tail): Differentiate the logarithm of the grand partition function along an interpolation. -> Range-independent pressure difference bound.
- **step-kac-limit** [claim](#stmt-prop-kac) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-kac): Box decomposition with upper and lower estimates, then convex upgrade to uniform convergence. -> Pressure with one long-range attraction converges to the transform.
- **step-reference-pressure** [claim](#stmt-prop-free-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-free-pressure): Cluster enumeration for free configurations and bad-edge removal for the upper bound; dilated packings of clusters for the lower bound. -> Three-branch limiting pressure.
- **step-ideal-planes** [claim](#stmt-lem-initial-transform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-initial-transform): Fenchel inequality for the upper limit and subgradient choice for the matching lower limit. -> Three-plane limit and localization of maximizing densities.
- **step-joint-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-joint-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-joint-support): Direct manipulation of the conjugate inequalities. -> Identification of first coordinates of joint supports.
- **step-transform-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-transform-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transform-support): Complete the square at a maximizing density and use the fixed-density branch below the transform. -> Supports of the pressure and of the transform at shifted fields.
- **step-initial-triple** [claim](#stmt-prop-initial-triple) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-initial-triple): Minimize the transform minus a linear function, then localize reachable gradients near the three plane gradients. -> Initial point with three separated groups of supporting slopes.
- **step-persistence** [claim](#stmt-lem-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-persistence): Rescale at the scale of the attractive mass, identify the limit as a support function plus a quadratic, then localize reachable gradients and argue by contradiction for a uniform threshold. -> Persistence of three gradient groups at a nearby point.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-23): Choose each mass before its range, pass the common ball of slopes to the physical pressure, and read a horizontal slice as two distinct canonical supergradients. -> One potential with a strict derivative jump at one inverse temperature on an open density interval.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-strict-temperature-jump**: One admissible radial potential and one critical inverse temperature give a strict downward jump of the one-sided inverse-temperature derivatives of the canonical free energy at every density of a nonempty open interval.
- **inv-tail-little-o**: The tail of the constructed potential is o(r^{-3}) only; no fixed power margin is obtained.
- **inv-shell-five-point-exclusion**: The narrow core shell contains no five mutually shell-distant points, which caps local clusters at four particles and yields a quadratic edge bound.
- **inv-uniform-stability**: The quadratic local occupancy lower bound on the energy holds with constants uniform over the whole class of attractive perturbations at the fixed scale.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-power-law-margin**: Use the construction where an algebraic decay margin of the tail is required. -> Only the little-o decay is proved; a power-law bound with a fixed positive exponent margin is not established, so downstream bounds that need it have no support.
- **anti-phase-identification**: Read the derivative jump as a proof of a conventional liquid, vapor or solid transition for physical fluids such as Lennard-Jones. -> The theorem concerns an engineered potential and a free-energy corner only; it says nothing about the nature of coexisting states or about the ordinary 12-6 potential.
- **anti-direct-implementation**: Implement the potential as a ready force field with explicit ranges and masses. -> Each new attractive mass and range is chosen by existence arguments from the previous stage; no explicit values are produced, so no executable parameter set follows.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is a pure existence statement: the potential is obtained by an infinite induction in which each attractive range is taken sufficiently large after the preceding mass is fixed, using compactness and limiting arguments, so no finite procedure with computable output is given. The theorem asserts 'There exist an admissible radial difference-only pair potential', not a computed object.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives an existence proof built from infinite limits and an uncomputed sequence of attraction ranges. No executable routine, library or interface follows from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The potential is engineered for a thermodynamic-limit statement. A finite-particle simulation can only mimic it, because the ranges are chosen non-explicitly and tend to infinity. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No checker, solver or analysis tool is specified. The convex-analysis lemmas are proof steps, not tool contracts. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction is non-constructive. The attraction masses and ranges are chosen by existence arguments with thresholds that are not computed, so there is no finite procedure with time or space bounds. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is defined. The cell partition is a proof device for occupancy bounds, not a storage scheme. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result has no computational kernel to map to hardware. It concerns limits of free energies, not arithmetic workloads. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary model or security property appears in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning, inference or dataset. The only link is the formal Fenchel-conjugate machinery, which is not a learning contribution. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Legendre-Fenchel duality and maximizing densities occur, but only as analytic tools for the thermodynamic limit. No optimisation problem is posed or solved for a user. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There are no nodes, messages or consistency requirements. The particles are a statistical-mechanics model, not agents. |
| other | True | weak | inspirational | stated | The paper can serve as a theoretical benchmark for testing numerical statistical-mechanics tools. The engineered potential has a divergent core, an attractive interval and a tail with $\phi(r)=o(r^{-3})$, and its free energy has a strict derivative jump in inverse temperature over a whole density interval. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-admissible-class** (hypothesis): The result concerns only potentials with a divergent core, a nontrivial attractive interval, an integrable tail and a stability constant. Potentials outside this admissible class are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the derivative-jump conclusion to a potential lacking the divergent core or the stability bound']
- **limit-engineered-potential** (formal_scope): The theorem concerns an engineered potential in the explicit integrable-tail class. It gives no transition for the ordinary 12-6 Lennard-Jones potential and does not identify the coexisting states as liquid, vapor or solid phases. Blocked use: ['Claiming a Lennard-Jones phase transition', 'Reading the derivative jump as a conventional liquid, vapor or solid transition']
- **limit-tail-no-power-margin** (formal_scope): The tail is only little-o of the inverse cube; no bound with a fixed positive power margin is established. Blocked use: ['Using the potential where a bound of order r^{-3-epsilon} with fixed epsilon is required']
- **limit-density-interval** (edge_case): The strict derivative jump is proved for densities in one open interval around a center determined by the packing density, not for all densities. Blocked use: ['Asserting a temperature corner at densities outside the interval I']
- **limit-large-core-scale** (hypothesis): The uniform estimates hold at a fixed sufficiently large core scale and need not stay uniform as that scale tends to infinity. Blocked use: ['Taking the core scale to infinity while keeping the constants of the physical class']
- **limit-nonconstructive-choices** (nonconstructive): Each attraction mass and range is chosen by an existence argument from the previous stage, and the persistence threshold comes from a contradiction argument. No explicit values are produced. Blocked use: ['Implementing the potential as a ready force field with explicit ranges and masses', 'Quoting a complexity bound for building the potential']
- **limit-kac-fixed-reference** (approximation): The Kac approximation fixes the finite reference first and then takes the new range large. No uniform rate over changing reference ranges is available. Blocked use: ['Assuming a uniform convergence rate of the Kac limit across varying reference ranges']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 228 (`lean/docs/228.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A continuum temperature singularity for a radial pair potential](../../preprints/A-continuum-temperature-singularity-for-a-radial-pair-potential-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A radial continuum phase transition with algebraic decay](../../preprints/A-radial-continuum-phase-transition-with-algebraic-decay-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization constructs one stable radial pair potential in three-dimensional continuum space with a divergent repulsive core and an integrable tail that is $o(r^{-3})$. Its canonical free energy exists for every positive inverse temperature and density. At one inverse temperature $\beta_c\in(1/2,3/2)$, the free energy has a strict finite jump between its one-sided derivatives with respect to inverse temperature, for every density in one nonempty open interval.
>
> The formalization constructs a bounded continuous stable radial pair potential in $\mathbb R^3$ satisfying $|\phi(r)|\le Cr^{-3-1/32}$ for $r\ge1$. There is a nonempty open interval of positive densities around $5p/3$, where $p$ is the unit-separated packing-density limit, on which the canonical free energy exists at every positive inverse temperature and has a strict downward derivative jump at one common $\beta_c\in[7/8,9/8]$.
>
> The earlier fixed-density statement at $5p/3$ is also retained. Both statements concern the derivative with respect to inverse temperature.

Comparator source `ContinuumTransition.lean` (2385 bytes, sha256 `703b04bfb80732ba60d9c55cc108ea94e9d5740174695cae4ca97f99aedf5512`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open scoped Topology BigOperators ENNReal
open Filter MeasureTheory Set

namespace ContinuumTemperature

abbrev Space := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)

noncomputable def radialPotential (φ : ℝ → ℝ) (x : Space) : EReal :=
  if x = 0 then ⊤ else (φ ‖x‖ : EReal)

noncomputable def energy (φ : ℝ → ℝ) {N : ℕ} (x : Fin N → Space) : EReal :=
  ∑ i : Fin N, ∑ j ∈ Finset.univ.filter (fun j : Fin N => i < j),
    radialPotential φ (x i - x j)

def Admissible (φ : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  Measurable (radialPotential φ) ∧
  (∀ a b : ℝ, 0 < a → a ≤ b →
    BddAbove (φ '' Icc a b) ∧ BddBelow (φ '' Icc a b)) ∧
  Tendsto φ (𝓝[>] (0 : ℝ)) atTop ∧
  (∃ r₁ r₂ a : ℝ, 0 < r₁ ∧ r₁ < r₂ ∧ 0 < a ∧
    ∀ r ∈ Icc r₁ r₂, φ r ≤ -a) ∧
  (∃ R : ℝ, 0 < R ∧ IntegrableOn (fun r : ℝ => r ^ 2 * |φ r|) (Ioi R)) ∧
  (∃ B : ℝ, ∀ (N : ℕ) (x : Fin N → Space),
    ((-B * (N : ℝ) : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ energy φ x)

def cube (L : ℝ) : Set Space :=
  {x | ∀ i, x i ∈ Icc (-L / 2) (L / 2)}

noncomputable def boltzmann (φ : ℝ → ℝ) (β : ℝ) {N : ℕ}
    (x : Fin N → Space) : ℝ :=
  if energy φ x = ⊤ then 0 else Real.exp (-β * (energy φ x).toReal)

noncomputable def partition (φ : ℝ → ℝ) (β L : ℝ) (N : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if N = 0 then 1 else
    (1 / (N.factorial : ℝ)) *
      ∫ x in {x : Fin N → Space | ∀ i, x i ∈ cube L}, boltzmann φ β x

def HasCanonicalFreeEnergy (φ : ℝ → ℝ) (f : ℝ → ℝ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ β ρ : ℝ, 0 < β → 0 < ρ →
    Tendsto (fun L : ℝ => L⁻¹ ^ 3 * Real.log (partition φ β L ⌊ρ * L ^ 3⌋₊))
      atTop (𝓝 (-β * f β ρ))

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (φ : ℝ → ℝ) (f : ℝ → ℝ → ℝ) (I : Set ℝ) (βc : ℝ),
    Admissible φ ∧
    Asymptotics.IsLittleO atTop φ (fun r : ℝ => r ^ (-3 : ℤ)) ∧
    HasCanonicalFreeEnergy φ f ∧
    IsOpen I ∧ OrdConnected I ∧ I.Nonempty ∧ I ⊆ Ioi 0 ∧
    βc ∈ Ioo (1 / 2 : ℝ) (3 / 2 : ℝ) ∧
    ∀ ρ ∈ I, ∃ dminus dplus : ℝ,
      HasDerivWithinAt (fun β => f β ρ) dminus (Iic βc) βc ∧
      HasDerivWithinAt (fun β => f β ρ) dplus (Ici βc) βc ∧
      dplus < dminus

theorem main_theorem : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end ContinuumTemperature

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a stable radial pair potential in three dimensions whose canonical free energy has a strict downward derivative jump at one common finite positive inverse temperature throughout an open interval of positive densities. The potential has a divergent repulsive core, a nontrivial attractive interval, and an integrable tail satisfying $`\phi(r)=o(r^{-3})`$. Its free-cube canonical free energy is finite at every positive inverse temperature and density.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
